# Laws — catalog

Two-level scheme + full law index. Lookup by `P<n>` or `L<n>`.

Status correction (2026-08-14): L371 now blocks only the mismatched peer's campaign entry while keeping
roster admission soft; L374 and L375 are executable and green. Older row prose saying that L371 lets the
peer play/readies or that L374/L375 are “NOT YET EXECUTABLE” is historical evidence, not current policy.

- `P<n>` = ARCHITECTURAL PRINCIPLE. Prose source `ARCHITECTURE.md`. **Its old wording "law N" means `P<N>`** — same numbers, renamed so `L` is the harness alone.
- `L<n>` = EXECUTABLE HARNESS LAW. `tools/RailCheck/L<n>_<Name>.cs`, or an inline method in `Program.cs` (marked *inline*). Run: `cd tools/RailCheck && dotnet run -c Debug` — exit 0 green, 1 red.
- `L-A`…`L-F` = boundary law, `docs/boundary-law.md`, letters, ride/refuse rules. Untouched here.
- NOT interchangeable: harness `L11` (no `LocalizedTextBind` rides covered) ≠ principle `P11` (UiEventMap repaint).
- Origin `incident` = header cites an observed failure (dated session / quoted report / log line / measured number / named breaking commit).
- Origin `principle` = reasoning, decompile, developer mandate or audit; no observed failure. A generic "this repo has paid for this class" is NOT incident evidence.
- Origin `unclear` = header does not say. Listed in Attention.
- Guard = literal tokens in the law's file: `premise-changed`, `POSITIVE CONTROL`, both, neither. See Attention — `neither` ≠ "checks nothing".

## Principles

- **P1 — TWO PRIMITIVES, AND NOTHING IS SILENT.** Intent (client→host gesture) + Delta (host→peers state) are the only two things on the wire; join/reconnect = native save transfer, not a delta; what a frame cannot carry is NAMED, never silently skipped.
  - pending amendment: 0xB6/0xB7 = third category, ephemeral presentation delta — `docs/design-event-windows.md` §4
  - laws: L34 L37 L39 L40 L41 L61 L62 L70 L71 L83 L93 L106 L107 L120 L122 L124 L125 L127 L128 L134 L135 L136 L142 L143 L321 L323 L324 L325 L374 L375
- **P2 — ONE PLACE NAMES THINGS.** `IdentityResolver` only; stable game ids; path grammar `root.Member.Member#elemKey`; resolved symmetrically on the client over its own graph; no stable key → subtree EXCLUDED + reported; never index-addressed.
  - laws: L10 L14 L17 L22 L28 L56 L58 L62 L101 L106 L107 L112 L113 L140 L374
- **P3 — THE IDENTITY BOUNDARY.** Rail projects VALUES. Create/destroy/re-identify = hand-written structural layer, the ONLY bespoke sync code allowed. Authoritative logic behind a native setter (reward chains, campaign writes, `Complete()`) is the host's. A projector may not write what a simulation mints.
  - laws: L2 L3 L11 L29 L30 L31 L35 L36 L49 L66 L67 L69 L77 L81 L96 L110 L121 L126 L128 L131 L137 L147 L320 L325 L326
- **P4a — BLOCK-FIRST INTENT SEAMS.** On a client outside `SyncApplyScope` a native state mutation is BLOCKED and converted to an intent; the HOST runs the native method; result mirrors back on the Delta rail. Presentation staging may proceed, model writes may not. One gate: `IntentRail.ShouldRunNative`.
  - laws: L12 L19 L24 L26 L32 L36 L42 L63 L65 L69 L76b L99 L110 L111 L138 L320
- **P4b — SIM GATING.** Client clock not frozen → host-only sim funnels prefix-skipped on the client (`LevelHourlyUpdateCrt`, `Research.Update`), reschedule preserved.
  - laws: L42 L63
- **P4c — PRESENTATION SEAMS ALTER NOTHING.** Replay what the model already says. Never block, never write, never throw into game code.
  - laws: L158 (written 2026-08-07, the same day the hole was ruled real — see Attention). Not "jointly defended with P11": P11 laws assert a repaint is REACHED, which a seam that also writes model state passes.
- **P5 — TACTICAL SCOPE IS SHARED AND CONCURRENT.** Six peers command six soldiers at once. First-to-act-wins, no ownership table, no ledger a reload can lose. Which peer plays a mission = tactical scope, not a permission.
  - laws: L27 L44 L45 L47 L65 L68 L75 L76a L78 L80 L97 L99 L104 L111 L123 L130 L132 L139 L140 L141 L144 L145 L146 L149 L165 L85 L320 L321
- **P6 — DETERMINISM / CANONICALITY.** Sorted roots/children/subkeys, fixed metadata field order, byte-identical deltas on both peers. Serializer blobs licensed ONLY as structural payloads.
  - laws: L2 L4 L5 L6 L7 L10 L13 L15 L16 L20 L34 L35 L43 L66 L73 L76a L103 L126 L128
- **P7 — CONVERGENCE.** Idempotence under redelivery; seq-gap → resync; CRC per path-subtree = drift backstop; reject → scoped `ForceReemit` + the family's reconverge. Never log-only.
  - laws: L1 L7 L8 L12 L13 L25 L27 L41 L44 L57 L58 L98 L100 L105 L123 L131 L133 L137 L146 L150 L152 L153 L375
- **P8 — APPLY SCOPE.** Every client apply wraps itself in `SyncApplyScope`: an apply never echoes back as an intent; apply-driven repaints stay suppressed at intent seams + the storage gates.
  - laws: L21 L51 L65
- **P9 — THE JOURNAL IS OBSERVATIONAL.** Written post-pipeline, never in the decision path; debug builds OK.
  - laws: none
- **P10 — PARITY BLOCKS CAMPAIGN ENTRY, NOT ROSTER ADMISSION.** Game build, Multiplayer build, host-required DLC, enabled mod set/version and synced settings must match before a peer receives a live campaign. A mismatched peer may keep a roster row and repair auto-applicable settings, but cannot ready or receive a mid-session save until the diff is empty. Other players never wait on it. An unresolvable def is LOUD, never silent.
  - laws: L108 L114
- **P11 — REPAINT IS THE RAIL'S OTHER HALF.** A mirrored value reaches the screen through the game's OWN read-direction refresh (`UiEventMap`, `UiNativeRepaint`) — never a hand-rolled paint, never a lifecycle transition where the model can express it.
  - laws: L18 L21 L26 L38 L45 L46 L47 L51 L52 L54 L60 L77 L79 L81 L83 L92 L93 L95 L96 L98 L101 L102 L104 L115 L116 L117 L118 L124 L127 L129 L130 L135 L138 L139 L141 L142 L144 L147 L148 L149 L151 L156 L163 L164 L165 L86 L323 L326
- **P12 — UNIVERSAL-FIRST, EXCEPT BY REPRESENTATION PROOF.** One generic mechanism per layer is the default. A bespoke state channel is permitted only when the committed coverage baseline proves the generic representation excludes the value, the channel still uses the shared envelope/sequence/dedup primitives, and an executable law covers authority, ordering and convergence. `GeoMarketplaceOffers` is the current reviewed exception; an informal subsystem copy is not.
  - laws: L32 L50 L99 L143 L155
- **P13 — NO IN-GAME QUORUMS, NOBODY KICKED.** After campaign entry, any active player can keep playing when every other peer is AFK or paused. A barrier may count only live participants and may wait only on work that ends by itself, never on a human action. Lobby readiness and an incompatible joiner's own campaign-entry gate are outside that scope; neither may block an already-running campaign.
  - laws: L26 L64 L71 L82 L84 L91 L94 L109 L118 L119 L120 L122 L129 L136 L143 L145 L150 L151 L155 L85 L86 L324
- **P14 — DRIFT IS THE GATE, AND A LAW ASSERTS AN OUTCOME.** `docs/rail-baseline.txt` + `docs/rail-contract.txt` = committed snapshots; ANY drift is harness-RED, so a field moving Excluded↔covered is a reviewable diff, never a side effect. A law asserts the executed OUTCOME, not the presence of a call, and carries its own non-vacuity.
  - laws: L6 L9 L22 L23 L33 L48 L49 L72 L103 L125 L163
- **P15 — DERIVED, NOT MIRRORED.** Unnumbered in `ARCHITECTURE.md`, asserted in the coverage rules. State a peer can recompute closed-form from already-mirrored inputs is RE-DERIVED locally, never shipped: mirror the ORDER, derive the pose. Inverse holds — what cannot be re-derived (mist) MUST ride.
  - laws: L43 L53 L55 L59 L73 L77 L102 L115 L154
- **P16 — FRAME BUDGET.** Unnumbered in `ARCHITECTURE.md`, asserted by the sliced walk. No unbudgeted graph walk on either peer; the rail never spends a frame the player is using; urgency never outbids local input. Also may not IDLE — a sim fact waits no longer than it must.
  - laws: L50 L74 L100 L153 L154 L156

| L81 | after a settle, a peer's visibility for that actor MATCHES the host's — the client stops deciding, and it can go DOWN as well as up; a mirrored stat write fires the native event | P11 P3 | incident | invisible enemy + missing health bar; `0c54378` shipped the repair with no arm. SUBJECT REPLACED 2026-08-08 on the owner's ruling that every peer's visibility must equal the host's: the old claim ("a peer re-runs its own vision at the settle") is now false by construction — the host's per-(looking faction, actor) `KnownState` rides the settle and is ASSIGNED onto the client's counters through `IncrementKnownCounter`:444 and `DecrementKnownCounters`:494-505. The re-run could never have converged and no arm could have caught it, because every arm it had was about a call that really was being made: it was MONOTONE (`KnownCounters.IncrementCounterTo`:55-67 is a maximum, so it could add a reveal the host had and never remove one the host lacked — half of the owner's "in BOTH directions" report), and the peers do not even share the geometry it was computed from (`SceneObjectIdsComponent.MergeWith`:29-34 re-mints a COLLIDING destructible guid at random per peer; 26 collisions over 52 objects in one measured mission, 63 over 126 in another). The stat half is unchanged. Falsified RED both ways (the `ApplyVision` call deleted from `ApplySettle` → `settle-blind`; the `DecrementKnownCounters` call deleted → `vision-raise-only`; a native `UpdateVisibilityOfAllTowardsActor` put back into `ApplyVision` → `vision-decided-locally`; `CollectVision` replaced by an empty list → `vision-not-shipped`), GREEN restored after each | both |

ARMS C-E REWRITTEN 2026-08-15, same intent, stronger form: they used to assert that `TimeAnchor.Canonical` reads `DiffEngine.LastCycleSeconds` at a rate — a HOST-side estimate of a flight the host cannot observe, which priced a whole publish cycle (0.4 s cap) against a real ~20 ms flight and at ~3600 game-s/real-s minted a CONSTANT client lead of `3600.00 x 0.4 = 1440.000` game-s, held 102 s across re-latches — i.e. the law was GREEN through the same clock error with the sign flipped. The anchor now publishes RAW host `Now` and `ApplyIfTouched` prices the delay THIS peer measured (`NoteBatchReceived` → end of the batch) at `ClientRate`. The arms EXECUTE `CompensatedAnchorSeconds` instead of witnessing an IL call: zero delay costs zero, the correction is linear in rate and delay, the clamp binds. Old arm E (`lag-unmeasured`) retired with its subject — `DiffEngine.LastCycleSeconds` had no consumer left and is DELETED. Semantic kill: `ApplyIfTouched` rebasing on the raw anchor → `L100 anchor-unpriced` RED, restored GREEN | neither |

| L128 | a baseline is a promise about ONE level | P6 P1 | incident | 2026-08-05: site COMPLETED on the host, still an active quest site on the clients; f15969 BASELINE swallowed it. RE-INVESTIGATED 2026-08-15 (host log 13:24:07.067, lobby-play entry, `boundary was taken on level #0 and this walk is on level #-18602`) — VERDICT: NOT A DEFECT, and L128 was green because the predicate already asserts exactly this row (`ask(0, 9001)` must EMIT). `#0` is not a level id but the sentinel `ResetForReloadBoundary`:455 stamps when `GeoLevel()` was null at the boundary, which is STRUCTURAL: the shared reload-entry hook `SaveTransferCoordinator.PrepareEntryFromBlobCrt`:1926 takes the boundary BEFORE it reads the save metadata or builds the level, so every lobby-play / save-load / on-demand-join entry stamps `#0`. The full-graph emit is the guarantee, not waste: the host completes the mission DURING the geoscape load (`GeoLevelController._missionToComplete`), i.e. after the blob the clients loaded. The tempting shortcut — adopt the first walked level as the boundary level — is NOT an optimisation: a tactical return ALSO takes its boundary with no level (HostTick bails while `GeoLevel()` is null for the whole battle), so it would baseline precisely the post-mission walk this law exists to stop. NO BEHAVIOUR CHANGE SHIPPED: only the log line was split so the `#0` case reads as EXPECTED instead of as a defect report, and the `baseline-on-no-level` arm text now says so. | premise-changed |

| L220 | the tactical READY CONTROL is reachable and a press leaves a mark: every method of `TacticalReadyButton` calls `GameObject.set_layer` at least as often as it constructs a `GameObject` (counted — the class builds TWO children), `TacticalReadySync.Toggle` reaches `Debug.Log` on the path that flips the flag, `TacticalReadyRowFollower` puts the reachability question to `EventSystem.RaycastAll` at the button's own centre and can reach `Debug.LogError` with the answer, and NO method of `TacticalReadyButton` sweeps the clone with `GetComponentsInChildren<Graphic>` while clearing `raycastTarget` | P4c P13 | live bug | 2026-08-08 owner: the co-op ready indicator next to End Turn took NEITHER hover NOR click, on the host and both clients. An undrawn graphic keeps `CanvasRenderer.absoluteDepth == -1`, and uGUI opens its raycast with `if (graphic.depth == -1 \|\| !graphic.raycastTarget \|\| graphic.canvasRenderer.cull) continue;` ("-1 means it hasn't been processed by the canvas, which means it isn't actually drawn" — `GraphicRaycaster.Raycast`, 2019.4). The REGRESSION is `30a64e3`: before it `TrimRaycast` bailed out ("its Button names no targetGraphic") and left all twelve NATIVE graphics live, and the button worked — proven by the owner reporting it ATE MAP CLICKS, which only a hit-testable widget can do. `30a64e3` silenced those twelve and substituted one hand-built face, and it died. The host ended holding one window the clients did not have, and it was the start-mission window for the mission all three had just finished. Careful about what this does NOT assert: L191 pins the opposite — restore KEPT COUNTS are MEANT to differ (the host restores its own blob, a client re-carries its own copy through `EventPopup.RequeueUnanswered`), and "make the counts agree" is a regression in both directions. The divergence measured here is DOWNSTREAM of the single L260 defect: the host kept a window it should also have dropped. So the convergence property is not equal counts but a verdict with no peer in it — two peers agree about a dead window the way two calculators agree about a sum, not by exchanging one. That is simultaneously the NO-QUORUM guarantee stated as an invariant: a rule with nothing peer-shaped in reach cannot be made to wait for anyone to ACT. Only one mechanism in the game does that and it is native — `Research.Update`:654-660 dumps the whole research wallet into `AddProgressToCurrentResearch(..., propagate: true)`, which walks `_researchQueue` handing leftover points down the line (Research.cs:736-748). The old gate (`ResearchSync.SimGatePatch`, a prefix on `Research.Update`) OPENED on `engine == null || !engine.IsActiveSession` — i.e. exactly in the unknown window (mid-load, mission boundary, a momentarily unset `Session.HostPeerId`), and one tick there is all the cascade needs, because the funnel above it also restamps `NextResearchUpdate` (GeoFaction.cs:1416) which the rail deliberately does NOT mirror (rail-baseline.txt:544) — so on a client that stamp is permanently stale and `CanUpdateResearch` is permanently satisfied. Fix: ONE gate, one level up, on the funnel; it never consults `IsActiveSession` (a peer with an engine that is not the host is a client, session predicate or not) and falls back to a level-scoped client latch when the engine is gone, so solo is untouched. `ResearchSync.SimGatePatch` was DELETED rather than kept — two gates on the same tick can disagree, and the narrower one opening for a frame was the defect. Applied in a POSTFIX on `GiveExperienceForObjectives` and NOT in `ApplyOutcome` -- the board lands ahead of `OpEnd`, i.e. before the client's own award, which would add on top of anything stamped there; the postfix still runs before `UIStateBattleSummary` is built, so the table (`UIModuleBattleSummary`:284/294/339) paints the host's numbers. L427 arm (d) was NARROWED in the same change from "any patch of `GiveExperienceForObjectives`" to "any Prefix/Transpiler": a postfix suppresses nothing, which is what that arm actually asserts. | L460 | a RE-SEEDED navigation leg starts from the HOST's departure epoch, never from local now: `GenericApplier.ReseedNavigation` must call `AnchorToHostDeparture` and must call it BEFORE `NavigationComponent.Navigate` (IL offsets, un-filtered `CallSites` overload), the anchor must read BOTH `GenericApplier._departureAnchors` and `GeoLevelController.Timing`, the pure `CoveredSeconds` is EXECUTED over four cases (live stamp -> `sharedNow - departure`; no stamp / future stamp / stamp older than the whole route -> refuse), `DepartureAnchorRail.Stamp` must be called from `VehicleSync.TravelRepaintPatch.Postfix` and must read `GeoLevelController.Timing` + `GeoVehicle.RangeRemaining`, `DiffEngine.Emit` must ship `DepartureAnchorRail.Snapshot`, and `ApplyDepartureGenerationTail` must install it | P1 P3 | live bug | 2026-08-14: non-player faction aircraft existed on every peer but their geoscape positions did not match at all; player aircraft looked fine. Pose is deliberately NOT mirrored (fbc9065, rail-baseline.txt `Pos`/`Rot`/`SurfacePos`/`SurfaceRot` = EXCLUDED derived pose) -- every peer re-derives motion from the mirrored ORDER. But BOTH start inputs of that derivation are LOCAL: `GeoNavComponent.NavigateRoutine` takes `startTime = Timing.Now` at leg start (:96, after the 5 s pre-roll :86) and `CalculatePath` starts from that peer's own `WorldPosition` (:69-72), and a client re-seeds only when the order delta LANDS -- so it departs late by latency x `Timing.Scale`, minutes to hours of geo time at geoscape speed. PLAYER craft hid it because every leg of theirs ends in the host's arrival snap (`ReseedNavigation`'s parked arm); foreign craft fly leg after leg with no snap and never reconverge. `NavigateRoutine` also writes the COVERED leaves locally off that wrong start -- `Travelling` false at :138, `RangeRemaining` at :116 -- which the diff can NEVER correct because nothing changed on the host, and those are the subtrees the host log reported as CRC backstop DIVERGED on V#1/V#2/V#4/V#7. Fix ships no pose and no periodic mirroring: the host stamps its departure on the LEVEL clock (the one `TimeAnchor` holds equal across peers -- a per-ACTOR clock is an unshared epoch, L77) plus its `RangeRemaining`, both ride a SECOND optional GeoRail tail section (`0xD8`, read only while bytes remain, the `ManufactureSync.HandleInbound` precedent) with its OWN key set because `DepartureGenerationRail` only advances behind `MissionSync.CaptureVehicleDeparture`, which bails on `CurrentSite == null` -- exactly the mid-route foreign departures this fix is for. The client then fast-forwards the leg by `sharedNow - departure` through the game's own `GeoActor.SetOrientedGlobeWorldPosition` (never a hand-written `localRotation`) and seeds the host's departure range, so its own `NavigateRoutine` reproduces the host's subtraction exactly; `Travelling` is re-asserted from `UnderTravelOrder`, which this file already treats as the host's own two arms. The 5 s pre-roll needs no correction -- the host paid it too. | L470 | the MIST BLOB fits the string leaf it rides on: the rail-side string-leaf read bound (`WireString.Blob`, used by the `LeafKind.String` arm of `RailMeta.DecodeLeaf`) must be >= `MistSync.MaxMistDataBytes` and <= `RailMeta.MaxReassembledBytes`, and a string of `MistSync.MaxMistDataBytes` must survive `RailMeta.EncodeLeaf` -> `DecodeLeaf` EXECUTED against the shipped codec | P1 | live bug | 2026-08-14: the mist layer NEVER reached the client. 35 client-side lines from 21:44 to session end, one per re-emit: `GenericApplier: apply failed M#mist.MistData: string: implausible length 67220`, the last at 119968 -- it grows with campaign progress, so once a save's mist passes 64 KB the leaf is permanently unappliable. Root cause: `MistState.MistData` is a plain `string` member (the game's own deflate+base64 of `_mistData`, ~40 KB early to ~900 KB late, MistSync.cs class doc) and the value rail read EVERY string leaf through `WireString.Prose` = 64 KB. The host saw the consequence rather than the cause: `CRC backstop: root 'M#mist' STILL diverged on peer 2 after a forced re-emit` -- its one heal spent on a subtree no re-emit could ever heal. Fix is READ-SIDE ONLY, wire format UNCHANGED: the leaf arm reads through `WireString.Blob`, which is not an invented number but `RailMeta.MaxReassembledBytes` (16 MB) restated -- a leaf's bytes reach that decoder only after `GenericApplier.Reassemble` has already refused anything larger, and the transport per-message limit (`NetworkMessage.MaxPayloadBytes` = 8 MB) is never approached because `DiffEngine.FragmentForWire` splits an oversize value into <=64 KB fragments regardless. The stream bound inside `MessageSerializer.ReadBoundedString` still refuses a declared length the buffer cannot back, so FA-0012 is intact and `Prose`/`Text`/`Key` are untouched. SEMANTIC KILL (compile-valid `src/` mutation): `WireString.Blob = Prose` -> `L470 blob-roundtrip` RED ("a 1048576-byte string leaf does not survive RailMeta.EncodeLeaf -> DecodeLeaf (string: implausible length 1048576)") plus `L470 bound-shape` RED; line restored -> baseline GREEN | EXECUTED positive control (the same 1 MB encoded leaf re-read at `WireString.Prose` must be REFUSED -- an unbounded reader would otherwise pass the round-trip arm at any size forever) |

Nothing diverged, but the line came from `ClientAiEvaluationGate`, a SECOND gate sited one door UPSTREAM of the funnel gate `ClientAiEvaluationSeamGate` (`AIEvaluationAbility.Activate`, added `69d75d2` for L320). Two verdicts over one decision is the shape this repo has already paid for twice: they cover different door sets and can drift apart, and the narrow one opening for a frame is invisible while the broad one still reports green. `Activate` is the funnel BOTH doors pass through -- `ExecuteAIEvaluationAbilities`:1257 reaches it via `TacticalAbility.Execute`:1159, `AIEvaluationStatus.OnApply`:17 calls it directly -- and `ExecuteAIEvaluation` is private with `Activate` as its only referrer (L320 arm (a)), so the narrow gate is REMOVED and this law keeps it removed. Removing it costs nothing: with `Activate` refused, `Execute`:1159-1164 reads `IsExecuting` false and returns at once, so `ExecuteAIEvaluationAbilities` walks its actor list doing nothing -- what the empty coroutine produced, minus the second verdict. FOUND BY THE SWEEP AND LEFT OPEN DELIBERATELY: `PanicAbility.Move`:75 asks `EvaluateAiActionAsync` for a run-to-safest-position action, so a panicking actor on a client DOES evaluate locally -- kept on the standing judgement that panic is a REACTION riding the ordinary 0x82 mirror, but now an ASSERTED member of the caller set rather than an unexamined gap. | L510 | EVERY UNSEEDED GEOSCAPE DRAW A CLIENT CAN STILL REACH IS BEHIND A GATE OR REVIEWED: every method reachable within 4 call levels of a declared CLIENT-LOCAL entry point, and not pruned at a bound client refusal gate, that calls `UnityEngine.Random.*` (bar `InitState`/`state`) or `System.Random.*` must be on the law's in-source `AllowList` with a reason — and an allow-list row the walk no longer reaches is RED too | P1 P3 | 2026-08-15, the RNG-closure audit; follows L451/L480 (the 2026-08-14 research cascade) | The recurring defect was never one RNG site, it was that each one was found IN A SESSION, patched POINTWISE, with nothing saying whether the next one existed. What IS single is the DECISION — `ClientAuthority.IsClient()` (L506) — so the closure is proved at BUILD time over the call graph. ENTRY SET (declared, 5): `GeoLevelController.LevelHourlyUpdateCrt`, `GeoPhoenixFaction.OnResearchCompleted` (GeoPhoenixFaction.cs:1069), `GeoPhoenixFaction.OnSiteFirstTimeVisited` (:1168), `GeoscapeEvent.CompleteEvent` (GeoscapeEvent.cs:86), `GeoFactionReward.Apply` (GeoFactionReward.cs:110). DRAW SET DERIVED, NEVER NAMED: only the two primitive entropy sources are listed, so the game's own wrappers (`UnityUtil.GetRandomElement`:416, `WeightedRandomElement`:437, `Shuffle`:478 — all of which bottom out in `Random.Range`) are found transitively and a NEW wrapper needs no edit. FRONTIER: the walk prunes at every Harmony target read off L506's eleven converted refusal gates, so a retargeted or unhooked gate re-opens it. WHAT IT DOES NOT COVER, stated in the law's own doc: the entry set is DECLARED ("which native methods a client actually runs" needs a runtime trace, not IL); reflection, field-held delegates and virtual dispatch beyond the IL token are not followed; depth stops at 4; tactical RNG is out of scope; and it says nothing about whether a bound gate actually refuses (that is L506/L480/L451). FINDINGS: three ungated draws, ALL under `GeoscapeEvent.CompleteEvent` — `UnityUtil.GetRandomElement` (GeoEventChoiceOutcome:421/:427/:548), `RangeDataInt.RandomValue` (:172/:245) and `FactionCharacterGenerator.GeneratePersonalAbilities` (via `CharacterGenerator.GenerateUnit`, GeoEventChoiceOutcome:294/:302, which MINTS a soldier root). All three ALLOW-LISTED, not gated, and the reason is in the law: that funnel is covered by a CAPTURE seam plus two instance-level blocks, none of which is a refusal gate — the choice click is blocked at its presentation seam (`EventSync.EventChoiceClientLock`, block-first per `IntentRail.ShouldRunNative`) and relayed as an 0xB4 answer the HOST resolves; the game's trigger-time auto-answer of a single-choice event (`GeoscapeEventSystem.OnEventTriggered`:651-655) is unreachable on a client because `GeoscapeEventRaiseGate` refuses `OnGeoscapeEvent`, its only caller (:622); and the mirrored-instance route (`UIModuleSiteEncounters.SelectChoice`:598) is closed by `EventPopup.MarkResolvedInstance` + `StubReward`. A refusal gate there would be HALF-GATING: `CompleteEvent` RETURNS the `GeoFactionReward` its callers immediately dereference (`SelectChoice`:604, `SetClosingEncounter`:357), so a prefix that skips it hands them null — a crash, where the three existing mechanisms cost at worst a stale value. The other four entries close cleanly: the haven-research draw (`GeoHavenResearchManager`:92/:94) is the only one they reach and `HavenResearchGate` prunes it. REACTIVITY: the law adds no replicated state — it is a build-time assertion over the game assembly, and every value it is about already repaints through the paths L480/EventPopup own (the haven panel's `AssignedResearch` rebuild, the 0xBD reward payload + 0xAC record deltas). FIX, one line at the shared funnel: `FlushOnHostGesture` now calls `OpenUiRepaint.MarkLocalGesture()` after `FlushNow()` (`src/Rail/DiffEngine.cs`), and `MarkLocalGesture() => MarkHudDirty()` (`src/Rail/OpenUiRepaint.cs`). SIBLINGS FIXED BY THE SAME LINE, not separately: manufacture (`ManufactureSync.ShouldRunNative`), aircraft dispatch/exploration (`VehicleSync.CaptureTravel`/`CaptureExplore`) and facility builds (`FacilitySync`'s five seams) all reach the same funnel, which is why the fix is there and not in `ResearchSync`. WHY THE HUD HALF AND NOT `MarkDirty()`: that seam is entered by every capture family on EVERY invocation -- `CaptureTravel` sees every faction's departure on the planet before any owner test (`DiffEngine.FlushNow`'s own note) -- so a pathless mark would bump every declared scope and re-enter the open view state at NPC-traffic rate, which is the L492/L548 teardown bought back. The strips gate themselves: the agenda strip on its ROW IDENTITY (`AgendaSignature`, L548) and the others on their declared scope, so an unmoved model costs one signature build. STATED CEILING: the scope-gated strips (info bar, crew, roster, site menu) still wait for a rail path, because a local gesture carries none -- upgrade path is to pass the gesture's own path once a seam knows it. OTHER PEERS UNCHANGED: they repaint through the apply path this law does not touch (`GenericApplier` / `IntentRail.HandleInbound` -> `MarkDirty`). NOT A QUORUM and not a poll: the mark is raised by the acting peer, for the acting peer, drained by the frame tick that already runs. | L551 | EVERY PEER LEAVES THE LOADING SCREEN AT THE SAME INSTANT, NOT ON ITS OWN PACKET -- the reveal is a DEADLINE the host ships, not a message peers race: (a) `SaveTransferCoordinator.Update` must not call `PerformDeferredLift` directly (a lift on the broadcast frame IS the head start) and must reach it only through `TickScheduledReveal`; (b) `OnRevealAll` must reach `ScheduleReveal` and must NOT reach the lift, and the host's own release must schedule itself too; (c) EXECUTED on `RevealSchedule.LeadMs` -- the lead must GROW with the measured RTT (a constant is a guess) and be clamped at both ends; (d) EXECUTED on `RevealSchedule.Due` -- inclusive, so a peer whose instant already passed lifts on its next frame, and never true before the instant; (e) P13 -- neither `ScheduleReveal` nor `TickScheduledReveal` may consult the roster, the progress tracker or any peer, so the deadline elapses by itself; (f) non-vacuity -- the wire really round-trips the (boundary, instant) pair | P1 P13 | incident | reported ~15 times ('whoever loads first gets in first'), first MEASURED 2026-08-15 20:43 on three machines: lift host 52.897 / s3 53.317 / s2 53.544, input live host 52.994 / s3 54.325 / s2 54.546 -- the host could act **1.33-1.55 s** before its clients and the client order tracked their LOAD order. ROOT CAUSE, and it is NOT the barrier: the host waits for every live roster slot's `LoadComplete` before broadcasting `RevealAll` (`SaveTransferCoordinator.Update` -> `_tracker.AllDone(_engine.Session.GetLiveRosterSlots())`) and that is correct. What followed it was not -- the same frame that broadcast also called `PerformDeferredLift`, so the host lifted at t0 while each client lifted at t0 + wire hop + one of its own post-load frames, and the input unlock (`RevealInputLock.Converge`, keyed on `_revealed`) trailed further still. WHY NO EXISTING LAW COULD SEE IT: L94/L143/L433/L513 all own the BARRIER -- who arms it, who may release it, that the release packet is bound to the host's authority, that no peer lifts BEFORE its boundary releases -- and all four were GREEN through this defect, correctly, because the barrier was doing its job. Nothing in the repo asserted the frame AFTER the release. FIX: `src/SaveTransfer/RevealSchedule.cs` (new, pure) + `ScheduleReveal`/`TickScheduledReveal`/`RevealClockNowMs`/`MaxLivePeerRttMs` in the coordinator. The host picks an instant on the HOST clock (`PingTable.TryHostNowMs` -- the same Stopwatch base `ObserveHostClock` maps peers onto, NOT `SaveTransferCoordinator.NowMs`, which is wall-clock UTC and differs machine to machine), ships it in the RevealAll payload's second field, and every peer INCLUDING THE HOST lifts when it arrives. MARGIN IS MEASURED, NOT GUESSED: `LeadMs(maxRtt) = clamp(maxRtt + 400, 300, 2500)` over the worst LIVE client RTT the session already samples (`SessionManager.Ping.GetPingMs`); full RTT rather than RTT/2 because the measurement above showed the wire hop was the smaller half -- a client is spending its first frames after a multi-second blocking level load. Floor 300 ms so the host can never lift on its own broadcast frame; ceiling 2500 ms because the lead is dead black screen for EVERYONE and a pathological ping must not buy unbounded darkness. INPUT IS IN THE SCHEME FOR FREE: the unlock is keyed on `_revealed`, which only `PerformDeferredLift` sets, so scheduling the lift schedules the unlock. LATE ARRIVAL: `Due` is `>=`, so a peer whose instant passed in flight lifts on its very next frame -- late, never early, never blocked; `ReplayRevealTo` deliberately re-sends the ALREADY-PASSED instant rather than minting a fresh one, so a resumed paused peer does not wait twice. NOT A QUORUM (P13, arm (e)): nothing here waits on a human ACTION; the instant elapses by itself and a silent, AFK, paused or departed peer cannot postpone it by one millisecond -- `MaxLivePeerRttMs` reads only live clients, so a peer that left cannot even widen the lead. A peer that never sampled the host clock at all reveals NOW rather than stranding itself. NEW-CAMPAIGN BOOTSTRAP SHARES THIS PATH: `ArmNewCampaignBootstrap` -> `BroadcastLoadBoundaryBegin('new-campaign')` -> the same transfer -> the same `AllDone` release; `SerializeRevealAll` has exactly two senders in `src/` (the AllDone release and `ReplayRevealTo`) and both are now scheduled. NO BARRIER LAW WEAKENED: L433's codec arm was renamed with the payload field (`serverTicks` -> `revealDueHostMs`) and asserts the same round trip; L124's same-call-sites arm still holds because `HostReseedAfterReveal` and `HostReplayIntroCinematic` moved TOGETHER into `TickScheduledReveal`. MUTATION KILL (compile-valid `src/` mutation, semantic): `PerformDeferredLift();` restored at the end of the AllDone release branch -> `L551 host-lifts-on-its-own-broadcast-frame: SaveTransferCoordinator.Update calls PerformDeferredLift directly ...`, `RAILCHECK RED - 1 executable law violation(s); --update cannot baseline them.`; reverted -> `RAILCHECK GREEN - laws-run=351/351 law-violations=0`. Step-1 RED against the code as it stood before the fix: `L551 host-lifts-on-its-own-broadcast-frame` (x2, including 'there is no SaveTransferCoordinator.TickScheduledReveal at all') + `L551 arrival-is-the-lift` (x2, including 'there is no SaveTransferCoordinator.ScheduleReveal'). RailSim carries the same property as an observable run in `the-reveal-is-one-instant-for-every-peer`, which compares the OLD lift-on-arrival policy against the new one over the same hop skew and fails if the old one no longer reproduces a >=300 ms spread | premise-changed + POSITIVE CONTROL |

## Rows vs registrations — why 155 rows and 135 registered laws

### Mutation verification policy

- Run `tools/mutation-runner.ps1` for the exhaustive 299/299 execution-to-RED check.
- Synthetic mutations are infrastructure checks, not semantic production coverage.
- Require compile-valid `src/` mutations for critical authority, barrier, codec, dedupe, ordering, parity, and lifecycle laws.
- Count a semantic kill only when the named law is RED and restoration returns GREEN.
- Add targeted mutations for critical bugs; do not maintain one production patch per law.

The two numbers count different things and always will. `tools/law-count.txt` counts REGISTRATIONS
(one `laws.AddRange(...)` line each); this table counts NUMBERED LAWS. The mapping is many-to-many
on the inline side. Nothing is missing and no row is invented — verified 2026-08-07.

- **files: 77 rows ↔ 77 registrations, exactly 1:1.** `L26, L77, L79, L80, L84, L91–L162` — one
  `L<n>_<Name>.cs`, one `AddRange`, one row.
- **inline: 79 rows ↔ 60 registrations.** 60 inline methods emit 78 distinct ids; the catalogue
  splits one of them in two, giving 79 rows.
  - ONE method carrying MANY ids is the whole surplus. `RoundTrip()` alone emits twelve —
    L4 L7 L8 L10 L12 L13 L14 L17 L18 L19 L20 L24. `StructuralDescendLaw` emits L29 + L30.
  - MANY methods sharing ONE id happens too, and the catalogue names the arms itself:
    L76a/L76b = `TacticalFunnelLaw` + `TacticalPayloadUseLaw` (both emit the bare string `"L76"` —
    the `a`/`b` suffix exists only here, never in the code); L69 = `InventoryAndDestructionLaw` +
    `…Part2` + `ValidateProbes`; L15 = `OwnerBackRefCodecLaw` + `OwnerBackRefLaw`;
    L28 = `RootOwnershipLaw` + `SubEntityRefArm`; L49 = `ModalCoverageLaw` + `OneProducerPerWindow`.
- **1 row is not executable at all:** L78, prose-only, no arm ever. See below.
- Arithmetic: 70 file rows + 79 inline rows + L78 = 150 rows. 70 files + 60 inline = 130
  registrations. The 20-row difference is 19 (inline ids beyond their methods) + 1 (L78).
- The `147 vs 127` figure in the earlier handoff was the same accounting one snapshot earlier
  (`files=67`, three fewer rows). The ratio, not the totals, is the fact.
- **Consequence for review:** an inline law's id is NOT a registration. Deleting `RoundTrip()`
  silently retires twelve numbered laws while `inline=` drops by one. `tools/law-integrity.ps1`
  cannot see that. Prefer a new `L<n>_<Name>.cs` file for anything new — it restores 1:1.

## Unassigned / retired numbers

- L78 — prose-only, no executable arm ever. Cited `src/Tactical/TacticalCommandSync.cs:2371/:2434/:3044`, and by L104 + L127. L104's header states it.
- L26 (old) — record-derived event backlog, deleted 2026-07-30 with the engine it asserted; number re-used by `L26_PauseAndOneShot.cs`.
- L85, L86 — ISSUED 2026-08-07 (`L85_RestartedHostStreamIsApplied.cs`, `L86_AnnouncedBoundaryHoldsItsAnnouncer.cs`). Taken from the never-issued block on purpose: five agents were minting laws in one tree that day and three successive max+1 picks collided.
- L87–L90 — never issued. No file, no method, no citation anywhere in the repo.
- L374 and L375 became executable on 2026-08-14: both have file-backed checks, registrations, premise guards and executed controls. L374 also forced the pre-player DefRepository lookup to fail closed; L375 checks the exact started-level instance and every apply/resend entry point.

## Durable-window inbox allocation clarification (2026-08-10)

- The approved DWI allocation was `DWI-01..DWI-26 -> L376..L401`. **L402 WAS created after all**, 2026-08-10, as `L402_ADepartedLastSourceClosesTheWindowEverywhere.cs` — placed with its family rather than in the free L87–L90 block, which would have scattered it. No L403 exists; the family then continued at `L404`..`L410` (see the two sections below).
- WITHDRAWN 2026-08-10, same day: the membership-epoch design below was never wired and has been removed. `L376`, `L397`, `L398`, `L399` and `L400` are DELETED — see the commit `refactor(inbox): remove epoch bookkeeping no session path reaches`. Do not reissue these numbers; `L<n>` numbering is sparse by design.
- WITHDRAWN 2026-08-10, same day, by the rest of the durable-window surgery (`a13c63c`, `9624919`) — five more DELETED, each because its subject no longer exists. Do not reissue these numbers either:
  - `L377` a receipt advances no inbox lifecycle — it asserted `DurableInboxReducer`'s transition table, which production deliberately contradicts (`src/Rail/DurableInboxReducer.cs:37-42`). The L127(d)/L182(d) trap: a law mandating a table the engine does not obey. Its receipt half (a transport ack advances nothing) survives in `L396`.
  - `L381` a first valid shared answer applies one durable effect — every subject it named was deleted with the effect-transaction machinery. The outcome it protected is still held by `L44` (the freeze gate) and `L45` (winner selected and clickable, losers non-interactable), which assert outcome rather than shape. `L382` is untouched and green.
  - `L379` a host order survives the native save blob — the mod writes no save root at all now, so no host order rides a save blob; host order reaches peers only over the wire codec, covered by `L385` and `L390`.
  - `L383` a canonical result never rebinds by list position — both halves were properties of the save codec. With no decode and a store that starts empty each session there is no list to rebind by and nothing stale to quarantine.
  - `L394` compaction needs proof no durable source can name it — compaction existed only to bound the journal; there is no journal, and `CanCompact`/`CompactionProof`/`DurableReferenceClass` had zero production callers even before, which is the only reason it was ever green.
- Why: reconnect does not exist in this mod. There is no session-restore path — the only mention anywhere in `src/Lobby` + `src/Transport` is a chat string (`SessionLifecycle.FormatReconnectedNotice`). `Enroll`/`EndMembership` had zero production callers, membership is DERIVED from the entries themselves, and `MemberPresence` was a five-valued enum holding exactly one value in production. The owner's decision is that if reconnect is ever added, all windows reset — everything routes through the host anyway, so there is no history to restore.
- `MembershipId` is now the player guid alone. There is no epoch, on the wire, in a save, or in memory.
- DWI-16/L391 SURVIVES and still covers monotonic lifecycle/tombstone rules, but **no longer across a campaign save** — the ledger is not saved. Its fixture is a fresh session store REBUILT from the same ledger, and `ec61557` renamed its arm slugs accordingly (`store-rebuild-lost-the-lifecycle-or-its-revision`, `valid-post-rebuild-monotonic-lifecycle-refused`, `stale-or-terminal-regression-accepted-after-rebuild`, `tombstone-did-not-beat-peer-update-after-rebuild`, `pre-rebuild-tombstone-refused`). Its enrollment scaffolding was replaced by seeding the ledger with a real entry, and its `EndMembership` arm was dropped with it.
- Also repaired rather than deleted, each keeping its whole subject: `L380`, `L385`, `L386`, `L388`, `L390`, `L393`, `L395`, `L396`. Save-round-trip fixtures became a direct store rebuild and the `WriteRecord` injection seam became the `ValidateCandidate` preflight. `L393` in particular stopped asserting a hand-written fifteen-value modal table and now DERIVES the family set from `DurableWindowRegistry.RoutedPresentations`.
- Native campaign save/load durability is **no longer a mod concern at all**: the mod writes nothing into any save, and the native `GeoscapeViewSwitchQuery` queue is the source of truth (ridden by `WindowQueueSync.RestoreDropsResolvedSubjects`, `src/Rail/WindowQueueSync.cs:988`). See `HANDOFF.md` §4.
- inline laws have no `L<n>_<Name>.cs`; grep `Program.cs` for the id string.

## Mission-start windows and post-mission resupply — the 2026-08-10 owner decisions

Three owner rulings, given in these words, now recorded so nobody re-derives them. Held by
`L404`..`L407` (file-backed, registered, `tools/law-count.txt` 208 -> 212; `L408`..`L410` from the
same day's audit work took it to **215**) plus the two arms rewritten in `L163` and `L164`.

- **"для каждого своё окно ТОЛЬКО У ОКОН У КОТОРЫХ ИДЁТ ПОДТВЕРЖДЕНИЕ НАЧАЛА МИССИИ, без блокировки
  выбора. остальные окна по правилам как уже есть."** Shared-answer FIRST-WINS stays the RULE
  (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-09-durable-window-inbox-design.md:89`, `HANDOFF.md:44`,
  `EventPopup.IsFrozen` `src/Rail/EventPopup.cs:1225`, L44/L45). The ONE additional exception is the
  MISSION-START CONFIRMATION, matched on its native raiser and not on a name. **CORRECTED 2026-08-10
  by `86c2f62`, after the 22:42-22:45 three-peer playtest of `2bab25d`: the window the player is
  actually shown for "НАЧАТЬ / ОТМЕНА" is NOT a `UIStateGeoModal` — no peer entered one — it is a
  plain geoscape EVENT (`PROG_AN0_MISS`, `UIStateGeoscapeEvent`), and one client sat behind buttons
  the shared first-wins freeze had greyed for 58 s.** So the family is carved out at BOTH raisers:
  - the EVENT, which is the live path: `EventPopup.NeverFrozenClass` / `IsMissionStartConfirmationEvent`
    (`src/Rail/EventPopup.cs:501`/`:504`) name any window with a mission-starting choice
    (`OutcomeStartMission.MissionTypeDef` — the same test `MissionArrivalNav` arms on, so TFTV and DLC
    content are in for free); `IsFrozen` exempts it (`:1292`) and `EventChoiceClientLock.Prefix`
    (`:1849`) answers it per peer — a decline closes only that peer's window and resolves nothing
    shared, a start still goes to the host.
  - the MODAL sibling, still live and still carved out: `ShowMissionBriefing` (GeoscapeView.cs:1903)
    pushes a `UIStateGeoModal` whose `ModalType` IS `GetMissionBriefModal(mission)` (:1724) over a live
    `GeoMission`, Confirm -> `ModalResultCallback`:825 -> `LaunchMission`:1043. Named by
    `GeoWindowCoverage.IsMissionStartConfirmationClass` (`src/Rail/GeoWindowCoverage.cs:478`), read at
    `MissionSync.cs:1719`, a strict subset of the older per-peer answer class — the `*Outcome` sibling
    (`GetMissionOutcomeModal`:1800) stays in that class and OUT of this one, because it starts nothing.

  No copy is frozen read-only and no peer's answer is replayed onto another peer's copy. **L404**, whose
  arm (e) was RE-AIMED rather than added: it used to assert `IsFrozen` "cannot name" the family, which
  is the wrong subject and the reason it stayed green over a broken game.
- **The cost of that, and the hard part:** several peers may confirm the same mission, so the LAUNCH
  behind the window must be idempotent. The key is the SUCCESSOR, not a flag —
  `MissionSync.MissionStartAlreadyCommitted` (`src/Rail/MissionSync.cs:416`) reads the replicated ledger
  for the shared `DeploymentPreparing` occurrence over the same stable mission subject, and
  `RunNativeMissionOfferAnswer`'s new `startCommitted` arm (`:440`) stops a late Confirm falling through
  to a second native `LaunchMission`. **L405.**
- **"а окно подготовки к высадке у всех на первое место перемещается если кто-то выбрал начать
  миссию."** Already true and now asserted rather than assumed: `DurableInboxStore.TryStartDeployment`
  (`src/Rail/DurableInboxStore.cs:99-101`) mints the preparation for EVERY member off ONE peer's
  Confirm, and `DurableWindowRegistry.PriorityOf` (`:318`) ranks it `Deployment`, the maximum, which
  `WindowOrder.DurablePriorityHead` (`:131`) sorts descending. The native side needs nothing —
  `ToDeploymentState`:596 already queues `UIStateRosterDeployment` at `int.MaxValue`, so the rank table
  deliberately does not name it. A PUSH, never a wait (P13). **L406.**
- **"Да, первым после миссии."** `ReplenishSync.ReplenishRank` 20 -> `int.MaxValue`
  (`src/Rail/ReplenishSync.cs:94`). 20 cleared the event family but lost to `UIStateGeoCutscene` (100);
  at the ceiling the resupply screen loses only to the post-mission outcome modal, which shares
  `int.MaxValue` and is queued first from the same arrival — i.e. the native order, you-won panel then
  resupply. The rank does NOT also promote the YANK: `UIStateReplenish` joined
  `WindowOrder.HeldTransitionStates` (`src/Rail/WindowOrder.cs:212`) exactly as
  `UIStateRosterDeployment` already had, so the 2026-08-07 ruling holds. `L163` arm (d) was rewritten
  from the SHAPE it asserted (`rank < TransitionPriority`) to that OUTCOME. **L407.**
- **The post-mission resupply race is fixed at the root, not by a bigger timeout.** A client's geoscape
  is rebuilt from the HOST'S mid-tactical save, so `GetMissingItems()` is empty at
  `UIStateInitial`:125 and `QueueReplenishState`:127 never runs. The host now states the fact: a
  POSTFIX on `GeoMission.Complete` (sole caller of `PostmissionReplenish`, GeoMission.cs:896) arms
  `ReplenishSync.HostPostMissionTick`, which broadcasts the empty-bodied one-way edge
  `SurfaceIds.GeoPostMissionCommit` (**0xB2**, a retired id reused) from `SyncEngine.Tick` IMMEDIATELY
  AFTER `DiffEngine.HostTick` — behind the batch carrying the writes. The transport is per-peer ordered,
  so a client holding that message has already applied them and asks the game's own question ONCE
  (`ReplenishSync.OnPostMissionWritesCommitted`). The 180-frame poll survives only as a bounded safety
  net for a host that never sends. NOT A QUORUM: a host machine event, never a human action. **L407**,
  and `L164` arm (b) widened one hop so it follows the shared `TryQueueReplenish`.

## Known gap next to L85 — the `last == 1` restart boundary (recorded 2026-08-10, NOT fixed)

- `SurfaceSeq.IsStreamRestart` (`src/Rail/SurfaceSeq.cs:52`) recognises a restart only for `last > 1`.
  If a surface's PREVIOUS battle emitted exactly ONE message (cursor left at 1), the next battle's seq 1
  reads as the duplicate the guard exists to drop: the first message of the new stream is swallowed with
  no log — the same silence L85 was written for, in the one boundary case L85 does not cover (its
  incident drives the restart from cursor 12; the law has no arm at this edge).
- LEFT ALONE ON PURPOSE, not overlooked. From the receiver's side `seq 1 against last 1` is literally the
  same wire fact whether it is a re-delivery of the old battle's only message or the first of the new
  one. Widening the comparison to `last >= 1` buys the rare case by double-applying EVERY ordinary first
  message a resend repeats — a worse trade, and unfalsifiable either way.
- The real fix is a per-battle EPOCH id in the envelope (bumped by `SurfaceSeq.Reset`, compared ahead of
  the seq). That is a wire change plus a law of its own. Do that; do not widen the comparison.

Two clauses landed narrower than this entry assumed and the law's header says so: clause (b) can only see DIRECT IL writes, because this seam set drives the native model almost entirely through `MethodInfo.Invoke` / `FieldInfo.SetValue`; clause (c) is asserted at the CONTAINMENT POINTS (`UiEventMap.Fire`, `OpenUiRepaint.Repaint`, `ResearchSync.PresentFromMirror`) plus "no uncontained caller of `UiNativeRepaint.TryRepaint`", because the helpers that actually invoke native code deliberately carry no catch of their own — demanding one per leaf would have been a style rule, not P4c. - ADDED 2026-08-15: `L553_OneWindowOneIdentityOneResolution` (files 292 -> 293, registrations 352 -> 353). Two general defects on the answer path, both measured, neither about any one window. (1) IDENTITY WAS NOT UNIQUE: `WindowQueueSync.Identity` was the window KIND plus the 0xB7 payload, and an asset-deploy payload's entity ref is EMPTY by construction (`src/Rail/GeoModalMirror.cs:349` — which contradicted the "RootRef always names one" comment at `:289`), so two manufactured aircraft produced the identical string and a stale copy answered later validated against the NEWER window: the host deployed the WRONG asset. The identity now carries the HOST JOURNAL POSITION of the raise (`WindowQueueSync.RaiseTagFor`/`TagRaise`, minted at the one seam every pushed window passes — `GeoWindowCoverageGate.Postfix` — and already shipped in every raise as `GeoModalMirror.Raise.JournalPos`, re-applied on the peer at `GeoModalMirror.RaiseMirrored`). No per-window table and no second id scheme: an untagged window gets NO identity and the send sites announce which refusal they hit (`WindowQueueSync.IdentityRefusal`). (2) ONE CHANNEL SAW THE QUEUE AND ITS SIBLING DID NOT: `HandleAdvance` answered a window still held in `_viewStateSwitchRequests` (L176), `HandleDeploy` read `_currentStateSwitchRequest` alone — and an asset-deployment prompt is raised FROM a screen, so on the host it is normally queued: every deploy answer was refused, the transport was never placed, the prompt wedged on every peer. Both channels now resolve through the one pure `WindowQueueSync.ResolveAnswerTarget(string, IList<string>, string)` and both take the window out of the queue through `TakeQueued` BEFORE the consequence runs (answer-once, with no second ledger — L523 — the existing durable carrier is released, not duplicated). RE-EXPRESSED, NOT WEAKENED: L109 arm (g) and the inline L82 identity row now RAISE the window they ask about (`TagRaise`) before asking, because an identity belongs to a RAISE and never to an object; L176 keeps all four arms verbatim (`HandleAdvance` still calls `AnswerQueued` directly, `AnswerQueued` still reads `WindowOrder.RequestsField` and `_dialogHandler` and still pops no screen). SEMANTIC KILLS (compile-valid `src/` mutations, each verified RED then restored): identity without the tag — the literal pre-fix spelling — -> `L553 identity-is-not-unique` + 30 `untagged-window-is-nameable` (31 violations); `HandleDeploy` resolving against the current slot alone — the pre-fix body — -> `L553 channel-skips-the-queue`; `pending.RemoveAt(at)` in place of `TakeQueued` -> `L553 answer-may-apply-twice`; the `TagRaise` call deleted from the coverage gate -> `L553 raise-is-untagged` + `tag-is-not-derived`. Restored -> `RAILCHECK GREEN — laws-run=353/353 law-violations=0`, `RAILSIM GREEN — scenarios=14/14` (two new properties: `two-raises-never-share-an-identity`, `every-channel-answers-a-held-window`).
